Proof debt

what each proof rests on (asserted base) — PROOF-DEBT.md

Proof Debt Ledger

Auto-generated by (proof-debt-ledger). 3480 proven theorem(s); each is certified by the kernel relative to the asserted facts listed as its outstanding base.

Reading an entry

A proof's bill is the set of asserted facts it ultimately leans on -- resolved transitively: a cited proven lemma contributes its own bill, a primitive axiom or definitional fact contributes nothing, and an asserted fact contributes itself. Each such fact is a leaf of the bill.

  • proven modulo 0 (an empty bill) -- the proof rests only on the trusted base (the fixed primitive axioms, the kernel inference rules, and definitions). Nothing asserted stands between it and the kernel; this is the strongest thing a proof can report, and it carries no trust tier.
  • proven modulo {a, b, ...} -- the proof is unconditional given a, b, ... Discharging every leaf (proving it, or making it a definition) would collapse the bill to 0.

The trust tier [trust: K] of a non-empty bill is the warrant of its weakest leaf -- one shaky leaf caps the whole proof. Tiers, weakest to strongest (from *warrant-kinds*; see the manual, ch. Classification):

tierthe weakest leaf is...
noneasserted with no warrant at all -- a backlog item, not a category
hand-wavea heuristic / plausibility argument, not rigorous
well-knowna standard textbook fact, asserted without argument
referencecites a specific named source
informala rigorous paper-proof exists, just not mechanised in VNB
proofa machine-checked VNB proof exists

So [trust: none] is the weakest report (some leaf justifies nothing), and modulo 0 -- no tier -- is the strongest.
